Why a GGUF's first decode step was slow, and what locking its dense matrices does to the decode after it: diagnosis runs with bench_diag.c, the harness with lines that say, with BENCH_DIAG=1, how many of the pages of the file's dense matrices are in memory (mincore; the head and the layers by quarters, in MB of 10^6 bytes, in memory / all) before each prompt, before and after the first decode step after it and, from set 10, at decode steps 32, 96 and 160; the system's page-ins (vm_statistics64: reads from disk into memory, pread's included, the file cache's hits not), and the experts read during the prompt, that step and, from set 7, steps 2 to 32 and 2 to the last. bench_diag.c is its last version: the earlier sets ran versions without the lines and variables added later (the steps after the first from set 7, the second mapping and the times of day at the end of the lines from set 9, the pages in memory during the decode from set 10, the lock to the end of the decode from set 12). Built as the manifests' command builds bench.c, with bench_diag.c in its place, against the change before's code (08293922; the two patches against its files, or this change's code, where a set says so). Each run: thermal state nominal, a 60 s rest, then /usr/bin/time -l env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin HOME=$HOME SNAP=<model|GGUF model> BENCH_DIAG=1 <the variables of the run> TEACHER=1 <harness> promessi_sposi.txt <lengths> <steps>. The harness output's columns are bench.c's: ctx_tokens, prefill_tokens, prefill_s, prefill_tps, gen_tokens, gen_first_ms (the first decode step), gen_steady_tps (steps 2 to the last), expert_miss, disk_s, fingerprint, tokens, nll, top1. From set 9 a script sampled the system's memory every second (vm_stat's pages active, inactive, file-backed, anonymous and occupied by the compressor, 16 KiB each, and the harness's resident size); the [memory] lines give their medians during each decode, the raw samples are in the raw logs (each line: time, then pages free, active, inactive, speculative, wired, purgeable, file-backed, anonymous, occupied by the compressor, page-ins since boot, and the resident size in KiB). A page-in count of 0 MB for a first step (sets 3 and 5 to 12) means that the system counted its reads late, in the next window.
